Corrigendum to ”Inverse Problems for abstract evolution equations II : higher order differentiability for viscoelasticity” Thanks: Funded by the Deutsche Forschungsgemeinschaft (DFG, German Research Foundation) – Project-ID 258734477 – SFB 1173.
Andreas Kirsch and Andreas Rieder

Abstract
. In our paper [SIAM J. Appl. Math. 79-6 (2019)] we considered full waveform inversion (FWI) in the viscoelastic regime. FWI entails the nonlinear inverse problem of recovering parameter functions of the viscoelastic wave equation from partial measurements of reflected wave fields. We have obtained explicit analytic expressions for the first and second order Fréchet derivatives and their adjoints (adjoint wave equations) of the underlying parameter-to- solution map. In the present manuscript we correct an error which occurred in a basic result and thus affected several other statements, which we have also corrected



2000 Mathematics Subject Classification
35F10, 35R30, 86A22

2.
Viscoelasticity

The viscoelastic wave equation in the
velocity stress formulation based on the generalized standard linear
solid (GSLS) rheology reads: In a Lipschitz domain
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
we
determine the velocity field
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\colon[0,T]\times D\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the stress tensor
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}\colon[0,T]\times D\to\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}}
, and memory tensors

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}_{l}\colon[0,T]\times D\to\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}}
,

l=1,\dotsc,L
, from the first-order system

(1a)

\displaystyle\rho\,\partial_{t}{\bf v}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{\,div\,}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}+{\bf f}

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D,

(1b)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}

\displaystyle=C\big((1+L\tau_{\text{\tiny S}})\mu_{0},(1+L\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\pi_{0}\big)\,\varepsilon({\bf v})+\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{l}

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D,

(1c)

\displaystyle-\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{l}

\displaystyle=C\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\,\varepsilon({\bf v})+{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{l},\qquad\qquad l=1,\dots,L,

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D.

Here,
{\bf f}
denotes the external volume force density and
\rho
is the mass density.
The linear maps
C(m,p)
for
m,p\in\mathbb{R}
are defined as

(2)

C(m,p)\colon\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}\to\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3},\qquad C(m,p){\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}}=2m\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}+(p-2m)\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}){\bf I},

with
{\bf I}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
being the identity matrix and
\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}})
denotes the trace of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
.
Further,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}})=\frac{1}{2}\big[(\nabla_{x}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}})^{\top}+\nabla_{x}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\big]

is the linearized strain rate. In formulation (
1
) two
independent GSLS are used to describe the propagation of pressure and shear waves (P- and
S-waves). The parameters
\mu_{0}
and
\pi_{0}

denote the relaxed
P- and S-wave modulus, respectively. Further,
\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}
and
\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}
are scaling factors for
the relaxed moduli. They have been introduced for the first time by

[
1
]
and are now widely used to quantify attenuation
and phase velocity dispersion in viscoelastic media, see e.g.
[
5
,
10
]
.

Wave propagation in viscoelastic media is frequency-dependent over a bounded frequency band with center frequency
\omega_{0}
. Within this band the Q-factor, which is the rate of the full energy over the dissipated energy, remains nearly constant.
This fact is used to determine the stress relaxation times
\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}>0
by a least-squares approach
[
2
,
3
]
where
up to
L=5
relaxation mechanisms may be required. Now we obtain the following frequency-dependent
phase velocities of P- and S-waves:

(3)

v^{2}_{\text{\tiny P}}=\frac{\pi_{0}}{\rho}(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha)\quad\text{and}\quad v^{2}_{\text{\tiny S}}=\frac{\mu_{0}}{\rho}(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha)\quad\text{with }\alpha=\alpha(\omega_{0})=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\frac{\omega_{0}^{2}\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}^{2}}{1+\omega_{0}^{2}\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}^{2}}.

Full waveform inversion (FWI) in seismic imaging entails the inverse problem of reconstructing the five spatially dependent parameters

(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})
from wave field measurements.

Using the transformation

\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\\
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{0}\\
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\\
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}_{l}\\
-\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},1}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}_{1}\\
\vdots\\
-\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},L}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\eta}$}}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

discovered and explored by Zeltmann
[
12
]
we reformulate (
1
) equivalently into

(4a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\bf v}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\rho}\mathrm{\,div\,}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{l}\Big)+\frac{1}{\rho}\,{\bf f}

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D,

(4b)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0}

\displaystyle=C\big(\mu_{0},\pi_{0}\big)\,\varepsilon({\bf v})

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D,

(4c)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}

\displaystyle=C\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\,\varepsilon({\bf v})-\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}}\,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l},\qquad l=1,\dots,L,

\displaystyle\text{in }[0,T]\times D.

Let
X=L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3})\times L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\rm sym})^{1+L}
. For suitable
2
2

2

A rigorous mathematical formulation will be given in
Section
4
below.

w=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L})\in X
we define
the operators
A
,
B
, and
Q
mapping into
X
by

(5)

Aw=-\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{\,div\,}\big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{l}\big)\\[2.84526pt]
\varepsilon(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}})\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\varepsilon(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}})\end{pmatrix},\ B^{-1}w=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\rho}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
C\big(\mu_{0},\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
C\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
C\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix},\ Qw=\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},1}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},L}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}.

With these operators the system (
4
) can be rewritten as

Bu^{\prime}(t)+Au(t)+BQu(t)=f(t)

where
u=({\bf v},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dotsc,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L})
and
f=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}})
.

Please note: The five parameters to be reconstructed by FWI enter only the operator
B
via, see (
3
),

(6)

\pi_{0}=\frac{\rho\,v^{2}_{\text{\tiny P}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{0}=\frac{\rho\,v^{2}_{\text{\tiny S}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha}.

3.
Abstract framework

We consider an abstract evolution equation in a Hilbert space
X
of the form

(7)

Bu^{\prime}(t)+Au(t)+BQu(t)=f(t),\quad t\in\,]0,T[,\quad u(0)=u_{0},

under the following general hypotheses:
T>0
,
u_{0}\in X
,

B
belongs to the Banach space
\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)=\{J\in\mathcal{L}(X):J^{*}=J\}
and satisfies
\langle Bx,x\rangle_{X}=\langle x,Bx\rangle_{X}\geq\beta\|x\|_{X}^{2}
for some
\beta>0
and for all
x\in X
,

A\colon\mathsf{D}(A)\subset X\to X
is a maximal monotone operator:
\langle Ax,x\rangle_{X}\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathsf{D}(A)
and

I+A\colon\mathsf{D}(A)\to X
is onto (
I
is the identity),

Q\in\mathcal{L}(X)
, and
f\in L^{1}([0,T],X)
.

Later we will show that the three operators from (
5
) are well defined and
satisfy our general hypotheses in a precise mathematical setting.

In
[
7
]
we explored (
7
) with
Q=0
.
Existence and regularity results of this paper apply correspondingly.
Let us be more precise: equation (
7
) can be transformed equivalently in

u^{\prime}(t)+(B^{-1}A+Q)u(t)=B^{-1}f(t),\quad t\in\,]0,T[,\quad u(0)=u_{0},

where
B^{-1}A
with
\mathsf{D}(B^{-1}A)=\mathsf{D}(A)
generates a contraction semigroup on

(X,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{B})
with weighted inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{B}:=\langle B\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{X}

where the induced norm
\|\cdot\|_{B}
is equivalent to the original norm on
X
. Further,
B^{-1}A+Q
is the infinitesimal
generator of a
C_{0}
-semigroup
\{S(t)\}_{t\geq 0}
with

\|S(t)\|_{B}\leq\exp(\|Q\|_{B}t),

see, e.g., Theorem 3.1.1 of
[
9
]
. Thus, (
7
) has a unique mild/weak solution in
\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)

given by

u(t)=S(t)u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}S(t-s)B^{-1}f(s)\,\mathrm{d}s.

On the basis of the above comments, both Theorems 2.4 and 2.6 of
[
7
]
carry over to (
7
) when we replace
f
by
B^{-1}f
and compensate the use of
\|\cdot\|_{X}
by an additional constant depending on
\|B\|
,
\|B^{-1}\|
,
\|Q\|
, and
T
. Thus, we have the continuous dependence of
u
on the data:

(8)

\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{X}+\|f\|_{L^{1}([0,T],X)}

as well as the following regularity result which has been
shown in
[
7
, Theorem 2.6]
for
Q=0
under more general assumptions on
f
and
u_{0}
.

Theorem 3.1
.

For some
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
f\in W^{k,1}([0,T],X)
with
f^{(\ell)}(0)=0
,
\ell=0,\ldots,k-1
(note that

f^{(\ell)}
is continuous). Let
B\in\mathsf{D}(F)
and let

u
be the unique mild solution of
(
7
) with
u_{0}=0
.
Then
u\in\mathcal{C}^{k}([0,T],X)\cap\mathcal{C}^{k-1}([0,T],\mathsf{D}(A))
and

(9)

\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{k}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{k,1}([0,T],X)}

where the constant depends on
T
,
Q
,
\beta_{-}
, and
\beta_{+}
.

3.1.
Abstract parameter-to-solution map

We define the following parameter-to-solution map related to (
7
):

(10)

F\colon\mathsf{D}(F)\subset\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)\to\mathcal{C}([0,T],X),\quad B\mapsto u,

where

\mathsf{D}(F)=\{B\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X):\beta_{-}\|x\|_{X}^{2}\leq\langle Bx,x\rangle_{X}\leq\beta_{+}\|x\|_{X}^{2}\}

for given
0<\beta_{-}<\beta_{+}<\infty
.

Transferring the techniques of proof of
[
7
, Theorem 3.6]
straightforwardly to
F
yields the following result.

Theorem 3.2
.

Let
T>0
,
f\in W^{1,1}\big([0,T],X\big)
, and
u_{0}\in\mathsf{D}(A)
. Then, the mild solution of (
7
) is a classical solution, i.e.,

{u}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}\bigl([0,T],X\bigr)\cap\mathcal{C}\bigl([0,T],\mathsf{D}(A)\bigr)
, and

F
is Fréchet differentiable at

{B}\in\operatorname{int}(\mathsf{D}(F))
with
F^{\prime}({B})H=\overline{u}
,
H\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)
, where
\overline{u}\in\mathcal{C}\bigl([0,T],X\bigr)
is the mild solution of

(11)

{B}\overline{u}^{\prime}(t)+A\overline{u}(t)+BQ\overline{u}(t)=-H({u}^{\prime}(t)+Qu(t)),\ t\in\,[0,T],\quad\overline{u}(0)=0.

The representation of the adjoint of the Fréchet derivative carries over as well, see
[
7
, Theorem 3.8]
.

Theorem 3.3
.

Under the notation and assumptions of Theorem
3.2
we have

\big[F^{\prime}({B})^{\ast}g\big]H=\int_{0}^{T}\big\langle H({u}^{\prime}(t)+Qu(t)),w(t)\big\rangle_{X}\mathrm{d}t,\quad g\in L^{2}([0,T],X),\ H\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X),

where
w\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)
is the mild solution of the
adjoint
evolution equation

(12)

{B}w^{\prime}(t)-A^{\ast}w(t)-Q^{*}Bw(t)=g(t),\ t\in\,[0,T],\quad w(T)=0.

Remark 3.4
.

Setting
\widetilde{w}(t)=w(T-t)
and
\widetilde{g}(t)=g(T-t)
we rewrite (
12
)
as initial value problem

B\widetilde{w}^{\prime}(t)+A^{\ast}\widetilde{w}(t)+Q^{*}B\widetilde{w}(t)=-\widetilde{g}(t),\ t\in\,[0,T],\quad\widetilde{w}(0)=0,

which is of the same structure as our original equation (
7
) since
A^{*}
is maximal monotone as well.
Further, in our concrete setting of the viscoelastic wave equation we have
A^{*}=-A
(see the next section) so that basically the same numerical solver can be used for the state and the adjoint state equation.

This remark applies also to the situation of Theorem
4.8
below.

Next we investigate second order differentiability of
F
.

Theorem 3.5
.

Let
f\in W^{3,1}([0,T],X)
,
u_{0}=0
, and
f(0)=f^{\prime}(0)=f^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
.
Then,
F
is twice Fréchet differentiable at

B\in\operatorname{int}(\mathsf{D}(F))
with
F^{\prime\prime}(B)[H_{1},H_{2}]=\overline{\overline{u}}
,
H_{i}\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)
,
i=1,2
, where
\overline{\overline{u}}\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)
is the mild (in fact the classical) solution of

(13)

B\overline{\overline{u}}\,^{\prime}(t)+(A+BQ)\overline{\overline{u}}(t)=-H_{1}({u}_{2}^{\prime}(t)+Q{u}_{2}(t))-H_{2}({u}_{1}^{\prime}(t)+Q{u}_{1}(t)),\quad\overline{\overline{u}}(0)=0.

Here,
u_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)\cap\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],\mathsf{D}(A))
,
i=1,2
, is the classical solution of
(
11
) with
H
replaced by
H_{i}
:

(14)

B{u}_{i}^{\prime}(t)+(A+BQ){u}_{i}(t)=-H_{i}(u^{\prime}(t)+Qu(t)),\quad{u}_{i}(0)=0,\quad i=1,2.

Further,
u\in\mathcal{C}^{3}([0,T],X)\cap\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],\mathsf{D}(A))
solves (
7
).

Proof.

We need to show that

\sup_{H_{2}\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)}\frac{\|F^{\prime}(B+H_{1})H_{2}-F^{\prime}(B)H_{2}-F^{\prime\prime}(B)[H_{1},H_{2}]\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}}{\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}}\xrightarrow{\,H_{1}\to 0\,}0.

Set
u_{+}:=F(B+H_{1})
and
\widetilde{u}:=F^{\prime}(B+H_{1})H_{2}
which is well defined for
H_{1}
sufficiently small. We have

\displaystyle B{u}_{i}^{\prime}+(A+BQ)u_{i}

\displaystyle\,=-H_{i}(u^{\prime}+Qu),\ i=1,2,

(
\ast
)

\displaystyle(B+H_{1})\widetilde{u}\,^{\prime}+\bigl(A+(B+H_{1})Q\bigr)\widetilde{u}

\displaystyle\,=-H_{2}(u^{\prime}_{+}+Qu_{+}),

\displaystyle B\overline{\overline{u}}\,^{\prime}+(A+BQ)\overline{\overline{u}}

\displaystyle\,=-H_{1}({u}_{2}^{\prime}+Q{u}_{2})-H_{2}({u}_{1}^{\prime}+Q{u}_{1}).

Then,
\widetilde{u}-u_{2}
and
v:=\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2}-\overline{\overline{u}}
satisfy

(15)

B(\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2})^{\prime}+(A+BQ)(\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2})=-H_{1}(\widetilde{u}\,^{\prime}+Q\widetilde{u})-H_{2}\big((u_{+}-u)^{\prime}+Q(u_{+}-u)\big)

and

Bv^{\prime}+(A+BQ)v=-H_{1}\bigl[(\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2})^{\prime}+Q(\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2})\bigr]\\
-H_{2}\big((u_{+}-u-u_{1})^{\prime}+Q(u_{+}-u-u_{1})\big),

respectively, with homogeneous initial conditions. Using the continuous dependence of
v
on the right hand side, see (
8
), we get

(16)

\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\,\|\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}+\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\,\|u_{+}-u-u_{1}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}.

Now we apply the regularity estimate (
9
) repeatedly for
k=1
to
\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2}
in (
15
),
then for
k=2
to
\widetilde{u}
as well as to
u_{+}-u
, which satisfies
B(u_{+}-u)^{\prime}+(A+BQ)(u_{+}-u)=H_{1}u_{+}^{\prime}
,
and finally for
k=3
to
u
:

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{u}-{u}_{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}+\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|u_{+}-u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}.

Let
d:=u_{+}-u-u_{1}
. Then,
Bd^{\prime}+(A+BQ)d=-H_{1}((u_{+}-u)^{\prime}+Q(u_{+}-u))
,
d(0)=0
. Again, (
9
) gives

\displaystyle\|d\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|u_{+}-u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}^{2}\|u\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}^{2}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}.

Substituting the latter two bounds into (
16
) yields

\frac{1}{\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}}\sup_{H_{2}\in\mathcal{L}(X)}\frac{\|\widetilde{u}-\overline{u}-\overline{\overline{u}}\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}}{\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}

which finishes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

In seismic exploration, where (
7
) is the viscoacoustic or viscoelastic wave equation, we can
assume the environment to be at rest before firing the source. In other words, the assumptions on
u_{0}
and
f
from the
above theorem are justified.

The symmetry of second order Fréchet derivative, see, e.g.,
[
4
, (8.12.2)]
, is clearly visible in (
13
).

The mindful reader might have noticed an unbalanced increase of the smoothness assumptions on
f
and
u_{0}
from
Theorem
3.2
(
f\in W^{1,1}
) to Theorem
3.5
(
f\in W^{3,1}
) compared to the increase of smoothness of
F
: two additional
differentiation orders for
f
gain only one order for
F
. This is because in (
16
) we need convergence of
\|\widetilde{u}-\overline{u}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}\to 0
as
H_{1}\to 0

uniformly
in
H_{2}
.
At least we get
F\in\mathcal{C}^{2,1}
, that is,
F^{\prime\prime}
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous.

Theorem 3.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
3.5
we have that
4
4

4

\mathcal{L}^{2}(V,W)

denotes the space of bounded bilinear mappings from
V
to
W
.

\|F^{\prime\prime}(B)-F^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{B})\|_{\mathcal{L}^{2}(\mathcal{L}^{*}(X),\mathcal{C}([0,T],X))}\lesssim\|B-\widetilde{B}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}

uniformly in
\operatorname{int}(\mathsf{D}(F))
. The constant in the above estimate only depends on
\beta_{-}
,

\beta_{+}
,
T
,
Q
, and
f
.

Proof.

Set
\delta B:=\widetilde{B}-B
.
For
H_{i}\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)
,
i=1,2
, we estimate

\|\overline{\overline{u}}-\overline{\overline{v}}\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}
where

\overline{\overline{v}}=F^{\prime\prime}(B+\delta B)[H_{1},H_{2}]
,
\overline{\overline{u}}=F^{\prime\prime}(B)[H_{1},H_{2}]
. From (
13
) we get

B(\overline{\overline{v}}\,^{\prime}-\overline{\overline{u}}\,^{\prime})+(A+BQ)(\overline{\overline{v}}-\overline{\overline{u}})=-H_{1}({v}_{2}^{\prime}-{u}_{2}^{\prime}+Q({v}_{2}-{u}_{2}))\\
-H_{2}({v}_{1}^{\prime}-{u}_{1}^{\prime}+Q({v}_{1}-{u}_{1}))-\delta B(\overline{\overline{v}}\,^{\prime}+Q\overline{\overline{v}})

where
{u}_{i}
is the solution of (
14
) and
{v}_{i}
solves (
14
) with
B
replaced by

B+\delta B
and
u
by
v
, the latter being the solution of (
7
) with
B+\delta B

instead of
B
and
v(0)=0
.
As before, by the continuous dependence on the right hand side,

(17)

\|\overline{\overline{v}}-\overline{\overline{u}}\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|{v}_{2}-{u}_{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}+\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|{v}_{1}-{u}_{1}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}\\
+\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|\overline{\overline{v}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}

where the involved constant only depends on
\beta_{-}
,
\beta_{+}
,
T
, and
Q
. All constants in this proof, which are not explicitly given, only depend on these four quantities.

Further, by applying (
9
) again repeatedly for
k=1
,
k=2
, and
k=3
, we obtain

(18)

\displaystyle\|\overline{\overline{v}}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|{v}_{2}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}+\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|{v}_{1}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}.

In view of (
17
) it remains to investigate
\|{v}_{i}-{u}_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}
,
i=1,2
.
We can use the same approach as above: Set
\overline{d}={v}_{i}-{u}_{i}
and
d=v-u
. Then,
\overline{d}(0)=0
and

B\overline{d}\,^{\prime}+(A+BQ)\overline{d}=-H_{i}(d^{\prime}+Qd)-\delta B({v}_{i}^{\prime}+Q{v_{i}}).

By (
9
) as well as the second and third estimate from (
18
),

\displaystyle\|\overline{d}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|H_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\big(\|d\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}+\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}\big).

We are left with estimating
\|d\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}
. Note that

Bd^{\prime}+(A+BQ)d=-\delta B(v^{\prime}+Qv)

and (
9
) delivers

\|d\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|v\|_{\mathcal{C}^{3}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}.

So we found that

\|{v}_{i}-{u}_{i}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\|H_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}.

Plugging this bound together with (
18
) into (
17
) results in

\sup_{H_{1},H_{2}\in{\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)}}\frac{\|\overline{\overline{v}}-\overline{\overline{u}}\|_{\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)}}{\|H_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\|H_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}}\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{3,1}([0,T],X)}\|\delta B\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}

and we are done.
∎

3.2.
Local ill-posedness

We recall briefly the concept of local ill-posedness from
[
6
]
:
Let
\Psi\colon\mathsf{D}(\Psi)\subset\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a mapping between infinite dimensional normed spaces.
Then, the equation
\Psi(\cdot)=y
is
locally ill-posed
at
x^{+}\in\mathsf{D}(\Psi)
if
in any neighborhood
U
of
x^{+}
there exists a sequence
\{\xi_{k}\}\subset U\cap\mathsf{D}(\Psi)
with

\lim_{k\to\infty}\|\Psi(\xi_{k})-y\|_{\mathcal{Y}}=0
but
\{\xi_{k}\}
does not converge to
x^{+}
in
\mathcal{X}
.

Here, we consider (
10
) as a mapping with the larger image space
L^{2}([0,T],X)
.
Theorem 4.1 of
[
7
]
applies directly to (
7
) and (
10
). The proof only needs a slight and obvious modification.

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
u
be the classical solution of (
7
) for

u_{0}\in\mathsf{D}(A)
and
f\in W^{1,1}([0,T],X)
. Then the equation
F(B)=u
is locally ill-posed at any

\widehat{B}\in\mathsf{D}(F)
satisfying
F(\widehat{B})=u
if for any
r\in(0,1]
there exists
\widehat{r}\in(0,r)
and a sequence of bounded, symmetric
and monotone operators
E_{k}\colon X\to X
such that
\widehat{B}+E_{k}\in\mathsf{D}(F)
,
\widehat{r}\leq\|E_{k}\|_{\mathcal{L}(X)}\leq r
for all

k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\lim_{k\to\infty}E_{k}v=0
for all
v\in X
.

4.
Application to the viscoelastic wave equation

We apply the abstract results to the viscoelastic wave equation in the formulation (
4
).
The underlying Hilbert space is

X=L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3})\times L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\rm sym})^{1+L}

with inner product

\big\langle(\mathbf{v},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dotsc,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L}),(\mathbf{w},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{l})\big\rangle_{X}=\int_{D}\Big(\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{w}+\sum_{l=0}^{L}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{l}:\boldsymbol{\psi}_{l}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}x

where the colon indicates the Frobenius inner product on
\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
.

To define the domain
\mathsf{D}(A)
of
A
(
5
) we split
the boundary
\partial D
of the bounded
Lipschitz domain
D
into disjoint parts
\partial D=\partial D_{D}\,\dot{\cup}\,\partial D_{N}
. Let
\mathbf{n}
be the outer normal vector on
\partial D_{N}
. Then,

\mathsf{D}(A)=\Big\{(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0},\dotsc\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L})\in H^{1}_{D}\times H(\mathrm{\,div\,})^{1+L}:\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{l}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{n}$}}=0\text{ on }\partial D_{N}\Big\}

with

H^{1}_{D}=\{\mathbf{v}\in H^{1}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3}):\mathbf{v}=0\text{ on }\partial D_{D}\}
and

H(\mathrm{\,div\,})=\bigl\{\boldsymbol{\sigma}\in L^{2}\bigl(D,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}}\bigr):\operatorname{div}\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{*,j}\in L^{2}(D),\ j=1,2,3\bigr\}
.
5
5

5

The traces

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{*,j}\cdot\mathbf{n}
exist in a suitable space, see, e.g.,
[
8
]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

The operator
A
as defined in (
5
) with
\mathsf{D}(A)\subset X
from above is maximal monotone.

Proof.

Since

\big\langle A(\mathbf{v},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dotsc,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L}),(\mathbf{w},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{l})\big\rangle_{X}=\int_{D}\Big[\mathrm{\,div\,}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}\Big)\cdot\mathbf{w}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}({\bf v}):\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\boldsymbol{\psi}_{l}\Big)\Big]\,\mathrm{d}x

we can proceed exactly as in the proof of Lemma 6.1 from
[
7
]
to show skew-symmetry of
A
. Hence,

\langle Aw,w\rangle_{X}=0
for all
w\in\mathsf{D}(A)
.

Next we show that
I+A
is onto adapting arguments of
[
7
]
. We will be brief therefore. For
(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{L})\in X
we need to find
({\bf v},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dotsc,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L})\in\mathsf{D}(A)
satisfying

{\bf v}-\mathrm{\,div\,}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}\Big)=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}({\bf v})=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{l},\quad l=0,\dotsc,L.

We multiply the equation on the left by a

\mathbf{w}\in H^{1}_{D}
, integrate over
D
and use the divergence theorem to get

\int_{D}\Big({\bf v}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}+\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}\Big):\nabla\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\Big)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{D}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\,\mathrm{d}x.

Now we sum up the
L+1
equations on the right, use the relation
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}({\bf v}):{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\nabla{\bf v}:{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}

for
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}}
, and arrive at

\int_{D}\big({\bf v}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}+(L+1)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}({\bf v}):\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}})\big)\mathrm{d}x=\int_{D}\Big(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}\cdot\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{l}:\nabla\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\Big)\mathrm{d}x\quad\text{for all }\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\in H^{1}_{D}.

This is a standard variational problem (cf. displacement ansatz in elasticity) admitting a unique solution
\mathbf{v}\in H^{1}_{D}
.

Set
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{l}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{l}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}({\bf v})
and follow
[
7
]
to verify
({\bf v},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dotsc,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L})\in\mathsf{D}(A)
.
∎

Next we show that
B\in\mathcal{L}(X)
from (
5
) is well defined with the required properties.
As in
[
7
]
we consider
C
of (
2
) as a mapping from
\mathsf{D}(C)=\bigl\{(m,p)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\underline{\mathrm{m}}\leq m\leq\overline{\mathrm{m}},\ \underline{\mathrm{p}}\leq p\leq\overline{\mathrm{p}}\bigr\}
into
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}})
6
6

6

This is the space of linear maps from
\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\text{sym}}
into itself (space of
automorphisms).
with constants
0<\underline{\mathrm{m}}<\overline{\mathrm{m}}
and

0<\underline{\mathrm{p}}<\overline{\mathrm{p}}
such that
3\underline{\mathrm{p}}>4\overline{\mathrm{m}}
.
7
7

7

Note that in
[
7
]
and
[
12
]
different
C
’s are used.
For
(m,p)\in\mathsf{D}(C)
,

(19)

\widetilde{C}(m,p):=C(m,p)^{-1}=C\left(\frac{1}{4m},\frac{p-m}{m(3p-4m)}\right).

Moreover,
C(m,p)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{N}$}}=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}:C(m,p)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{N}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{N}$}}
and

\min\{2\underline{\mathrm{m}},3\underline{\mathrm{p}}-4\overline{\mathrm{m}}\}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}\leq C(m,p)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}\leq\max\{2\overline{\mathrm{m}},3\overline{\mathrm{p}}-4\underline{\mathrm{m}}\}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}:\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{M}$}},

see, e.g.,
[
12
, Lemma 50]
.
Provided
\rho(x)>0
,
\big(\mu_{0}(x),\pi_{0}(x)\big),\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}(x)\mu_{0}(x),\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}(x)\pi_{0}(x)\big)\in\mathsf{D}(C)
for almost all
x\in D
we conclude that

(20)

B\!\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}\!=\!\begin{pmatrix}{\rho}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\widetilde{C}\big(\mu_{0},\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
\widetilde{C}\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\widetilde{C}\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0}\big)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}

yielding a uniformly positive

B\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)
in the sense of our general hypotheses from the beginning of Section
3
. Hence, the general hypotheses are satisfied for the viscoelastic wave equation.

4.1.
Full waveform forward operator

In FWI the five parameters
(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})
are of interest. Therefore we will define a parameter-to-solution map
\Phi
which takes these
parameters as arguments. A physically meaningful domain of definition for
\Phi
is

\mathsf{D}(\Phi)=\big\{(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\in L^{\infty}(D)^{5}:\rho_{\min}\leq\rho(\cdot)\leq\rho_{\max},\ v_{\text{\tiny P},\min}\leq v_{\text{\tiny P}}(\cdot)\leq v_{\text{\tiny P},\max},\\
v_{\text{\tiny S},\min}\leq v_{\text{\tiny S}}(\cdot)\leq v_{\text{\tiny S},\max},\ \tau_{\text{\tiny P},\min}\leq\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}(\cdot)\leq\tau_{\text{\tiny P},\max},\ \tau_{\text{\tiny S},\min}\leq\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}(\cdot)\leq\tau_{\text{\tiny S},\max}\ \text{a.e.\ in }D\big\}

with suitable positive bounds
0<\rho_{\min}<\rho_{\max}<\infty
, etc.

In view of (
3
) we set

\mu_{\min}:=\frac{\rho_{\min}\,v_{\text{\tiny S},\min}^{2}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S},\max}\alpha}\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{\max}:=\frac{\rho_{\max}\,v_{\text{\tiny S},\max}^{2}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S},\min}\alpha}

which are induced lower and upper bounds for
\mu_{0}
.
We set the bounds
\pi_{\min}
and
\pi_{\max}
for
\pi_{0}
accordingly by replacing
S
by
P
.
Next we define

\underline{\mathrm{p}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{p}}
,
\underline{\mathrm{m}}
, and
\overline{\mathrm{m}}
such that

(\mu_{0},\pi_{0}),(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0})
as functions of

(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny P}},v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}})\in\mathsf{D}(\Phi)
are in
\mathsf{D}(C)
. Indeed,

\underline{\mathrm{p}}:=\pi_{\min}\,\min\{1,\tau_{\text{\tiny P},\min}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\mathrm{p}}:=\pi_{\max}\,\max\{1,\tau_{\text{\tiny P},\max}\}

with
\underline{\mathrm{m}}
and
\overline{\mathrm{m}}
set correspondingly will do the job. The restriction
3\underline{\mathrm{p}}>4\overline{\mathrm{m}}
translates into

\frac{4}{3}\;\frac{\rho_{\max}}{\rho_{\min}}\;\frac{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P},\max}\alpha}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S},\min}\alpha}\;\frac{\max\{1,\tau_{\text{\tiny S},\max}\}}{\min\{1,\tau_{\text{\tiny P},\min}\}}<\frac{v_{\text{\tiny P},\min}^{2}}{v_{\text{\tiny S},\max}^{2}}

which reflects in a way the physical fact that pressure waves propagate considerably faster than shear waves.

For
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{f}$}}\in W^{1,1}([0,T],L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3}))
and
u_{0}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}(0),{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0}(0),\dots,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L}(0))\in\mathsf{D}(A)
the

full waveform forward operator

\Phi\colon\mathsf{D}(\Phi)\subset L^{\infty}(D)^{5}\to L^{2}([0,T],X),\quad(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\mapsto(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L}),

is well defined where
(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L})
is the unique classical solution of (
4
) with initial value
u_{0}
.

To benefit from our abstract results we factorize
\Phi=F\circ V
where
F
is as in (
10
) and

V\colon\mathsf{D}(\Phi)\subset L^{\infty}(D)^{5}\to\mathcal{L}^{*}(X),\quad(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\mapsto B,

where
B
is defined in (
20
) via (
6
).

Remark 4.2
.

Note that the image of
V
is in
\mathsf{D}(F)
by an appropriate choice of

\beta_{-}
and
\beta_{+}
in terms of
\rho_{\min}
,
\rho_{\max}
,
\underline{\mathrm{p}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{p}}
,

\underline{\mathrm{m}}
, and
\overline{\mathrm{m}}
.

The inverse problem of FWI in the viscoelastic regime is locally ill-posed. This can be proved using Theorem
3.8
, compare the proof of Theorem 6.7 of
[
7
]
. We give a direct proof though.

Theorem 4.3
.

The inverse problem
\Phi(\cdot)=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}},{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{0},\dots,{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{L})
is locally ill-posed at any
interior point of
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}=(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\in\mathsf{D}(\Phi)
.

Proof.

Fix a point
\xi\in D
and define balls
K_{n}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|y-\xi|\leq\delta/n\}
with a
\delta>0
so small that
K_{n}\subset D

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\chi_{n}
be the indicator function of
K_{n}
. Further, for any
r>0
such that

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n}:=\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}+r(\chi_{n},\chi_{n},\chi_{n},\chi_{n},\chi_{n})\in\mathsf{D}(\Phi)
we have that
\|\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n}-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}\|_{L^{\infty}(D)^{5}}=r
, that is,

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n}
does not converge to
\textstyle\mathbf{p}
. However,
\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\Phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})-\Phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\|_{L^{2}([0,T],X)}=0
as we demonstrate now.

Let
u_{n}=\Phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})
and
u=\Phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})
. Then,
d_{n}=u_{n}-u
satisfies

V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})d_{n}^{\prime}+Ad_{n}+V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})Qd_{n}=\big(V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})-V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})\big)(u^{\prime}+Qu),\quad d_{n}(0)=0.

By the continuous dependence of
d_{n}
on the data, see (
8
), we obtain

\|d_{n}\|_{L^{2}([0,T],X)}\lesssim\big\|\big(V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})-V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})\big)(u^{\prime}+Qu)\big\|_{L^{1}([0,T],X)}

where the constant is independent of
n
, see Remark
4.2
. Next one shows

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|\big(V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})-V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})\big)v\|_{X}=0
for any
v\in X
using
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n}\to\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}
pointwise
a.e. in
D
as
n\to\infty
and the dominated convergence theorem.
Since
\|V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})\|_{X}\lesssim 1
for all

n\in\mathbb{N}
a further application of the dominated convergence theorem with respect to the time domain yields

\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\big(V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})-V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}_{n})\big)\big(u^{\prime}(t)+Qu(t)\big)\big\|_{X}\mathrm{d}t\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0

and finishes the proof.
∎

4.2.
First order differentiability

To derive the first order Fréchet derivative of
\Phi
we provide the Fréchet derivative of

V
. Its formulation needs the derivative of
\widetilde{C}
which we take from

[
7
, Lemma 6.3]
:

(21)

\widetilde{C}^{\prime}(m,p)\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{m}\\
\widehat{p}\end{bmatrix}=-\widetilde{C}(m,p)\circ C(\widehat{m},\widehat{p})\circ\widetilde{C}(m,p)

for
(m,p)\in\operatorname{int}(\mathsf{D}(C))
and
(\widehat{m},\widehat{p})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Let
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}=(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\in\operatorname{int}(\mathsf{D}(\Phi))
and

\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}=(\widehat{\rho},\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny S}},\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S}},\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny P}},\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny P}})\in L^{\infty}(D)^{5}
. Then,
V^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}\in\mathcal{L}^{*}(X)
is given by

(22)

V^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}\!\begin{pmatrix}\!\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\!\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{\rho}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
-\frac{\widehat{\rho}}{\rho^{2}}\widetilde{C}(\mu,\pi)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}+\frac{1}{\rho}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}(\mu,\pi)\!\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{\mu}\\[2.84526pt]
\widetilde{\pi}\end{bmatrix}\!\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{0}\\[17.07164pt]
-\frac{\widehat{\rho}}{\rho^{2}}\widetilde{C}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}+\frac{1}{\rho}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\!\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\mu}\\[2.84526pt]
\widehat{\pi}\end{bmatrix}\!\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
-\frac{\widehat{\rho}}{\rho^{2}}\widetilde{C}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}+\frac{1}{\rho}\widetilde{C}^{\prime}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\!\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\mu}\\[2.84526pt]
\widehat{\pi}\end{bmatrix}\!\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\psi}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}

where
\mu=\mu_{0}/\rho
,
\pi=\pi_{0}/\rho
, see (
6
), and

(23)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{2v_{\text{\tiny S}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha}\,\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny S}}-\frac{\alpha\,v_{\text{\tiny S}}^{2}}{(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha)^{2}}\,\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S}},

\displaystyle\qquad\widetilde{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2v_{\text{\tiny P}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha}\,\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny P}}-\frac{\alpha\,v_{\text{\tiny P}}^{2}}{(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha)^{2}}\,\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny P}},

(24)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mu}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\,v_{\text{\tiny S}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha}\,\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny S}}+\frac{v_{\text{\tiny S}}^{2}}{(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\alpha)^{2}}\,\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S}},

\displaystyle\qquad\widehat{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\,v_{\text{\tiny P}}}{1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha}\,\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny P}}+\frac{v_{\text{\tiny P}}^{2}}{(1+\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\alpha)^{2}}\,\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny P}}.

Theorem 4.4
.

Under the assumptions made in this section the
full waveform forward operator

\Phi
is Fréchet differentiable at any interior point

\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}=(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})
of
\mathsf{D}(\Phi)
: For

\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}=(\widehat{\rho},\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny S}},\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S}},\widehat{v}_{\text{\tiny P}},\widehat{\tau}_{\text{\tiny P}})\in L^{\infty}(D)^{5}
we have
\Phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}=\overline{u}
where
\overline{u}=(\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}},\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{0},\dotsc,\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{L})\in\mathcal{C}([0,T],X)
with
\overline{u}(0)=0
is the mild solution of

(25a)

\displaystyle\rho\,\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{\,div\,}\Big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{l}\Big)-\widehat{\rho}\,\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}},

(25b)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{0}

\displaystyle=C(\mu_{0},\pi_{0})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}})+\big(\widehat{\rho}\,C(\mu,\pi)+\rho\,C(\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{\pi})\big)\varepsilon(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}),

(25c)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{l}

\displaystyle=C(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu_{0},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi_{0})\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},l}}\,\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{l}+\big(\widehat{\rho}\,C(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)+\rho\,C(\widehat{\mu},\widehat{\pi})\big)\varepsilon(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}),\quad l=1,\dotsc,L,

where
(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{L})
is the classical solution of (
4
).

Proof.

We apply Theorem
3.2
to
\Phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}=F^{\prime}(V(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}))V^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}
and get the system

\begin{pmatrix}{\rho}\,\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}}\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\rho}\,\widetilde{C}(\mu,\pi)\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\rho}\,\widetilde{C}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\rho}\,\widetilde{C}(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi)\partial_{t}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{\,div\,}\big(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{l}\big)\\[2.84526pt]
\varepsilon(\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}})\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\varepsilon(\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}})\end{pmatrix}-\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\rho\,\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},1}}\,\widetilde{C}\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi\big)\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\rho\,\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},L}}\,\widetilde{C}\big(\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}\mu,\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}\pi\big)\overline{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}\\
-V^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})\widehat{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}}\left[\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{0}\\[2.84526pt]
\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}+\begin{pmatrix}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{0}$}}\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},1}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{1}\\[2.84526pt]
\vdots\\[2.84526pt]
\frac{1}{\tau_{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},L}}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\sigma}$}}_{L}\end{pmatrix}\right]

which is equivalent to (
25
) in view of (
4b
), (
4c
), (
21
), and (
22
).
∎

Theorem 4.5
.

The assumptions are as in Theorem
4.4
. Then,
the adjoint
\Phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})^{*}\in\mathcal{L}\big(L^{2}([0,T],X),(L^{\infty}(D)^{5})^{\prime}\big)
at
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}=(\rho,v_{\text{\tiny S}},\tau_{\text{\tiny S}},v_{\text{\tiny P}},\tau_{\text{\tiny P}})\in\mathsf{D}(\Phi)
is given by

\Phi^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{p}$}})^{*}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}=\begin{pmatrix}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\partial_{t}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\cdot{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{w}$}}}-\frac{1}{\rho}\,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}):({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}}_{0}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}})\big)\mathrm{d}t\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{2}{v_{\text{\tiny S}}}\int_{0}^{T}\big(-\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}):({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varphi}$}}}_{0}+\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}})+\pi\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}^{v})\mathrm{\,div\,}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\big)\mathrm{d}t\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{1}{1+\alpha\tau_{\text{\tiny S}}}\int_{0}^{T}\big(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\varepsilon}$}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}):\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}^{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S},2}+\pi\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}^{\tau}_{\text{\tiny S},1})\mathrm{\,div\,}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\big)\mathrm{d}t\\[5.69054pt]
-\frac{2\pi}{v_{\text{\tiny P}}}\int_{0}^{T}\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}^{v})\mathrm{\,div\,}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\,\mathrm{d}t\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{\pi}{1+\alpha\tau_{\text{\tiny P}}}\int_{0}^{T}\operatorname{tr}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{\Sigma}$}}^{\tau}_{\text{\tiny P}})\mathrm{\,div\,}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{v}$}}\,\mathrm{d}t\end{pmatrix}\in L^{1}(D)^{5}

for
\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}=(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{-1},\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{0},\dotsc,\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{g}$}}_{L})\in L^{2}\big([0,T],L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3})\times L^{2}(D,\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}_{\rm sym})^{1+L}\big)

where
\textstyle\mathbf{v}
is the first component of the solution of (
